Provided that nothing in this sub-section shall apply to a
prosecution by a Company of any of its officer;
Provided further that in specifying the sum required to be paid
or credited for the compounding of an offence under this sub-
section, the sum, if any, paid by way of additional fee under sub-
section ( 2) of section 611 shall be taken into account.
(2) Nothing in sub-section ( 1) shall apply to an offence
committed by a company or its officer within a period of three
years from the date on which a similar offence committed by it or
him was compound under this section.
Explanation—  For the purpose of this section,—
(a) any second or subsequent offence committed after
their expiry of period of three years from the date on which the
offence was previously compounded, shall be deemed to be a
first offence;
(b) “Regional Director” means a person appointed by the
Central Government as a Regional Director for the purposes of
this Act.
(3) Every Regional Director shall exercise the powers to
compound an offence, subject to the direction, control and
supervision of the Company Law Board.
(4) ( a) Every application for the compounding of an offence
shall be made to the Registrar who shall forward the same, together
with his comments thereon, to the Company Law Board or the
Regional Director, as the case may be.
(b) Where any offence is compounded, under this section,
whether before or after the institution of any prosecution, an
Composition
of certain
offences.

264
intimation thereof shall be given by the company to the Registrar
within seven days from the date on which the offence is so
compounded.
(c) Where any offence is compounded before the institution of
any prosecution, no prosecution shall be instituted in relation to
such offence, either by the Registrar or by any shareholder of the
company or by any person authorized by the Central Government
against the offender in relation to whom the offence is so compounded.
(d) Where the Composition of any offence is made after the
institution of any prosecution, such composition shall be brought by
the Registrar in writing, to the notice of the Court in which the
prosecution is pending and on such notice of the composition of the
offence being given, the company or its officer in relation to whom
the offence is so compounded shall be discharged.
(5) The Company Law Board or the Regional Director, as the
case may be, while dealing with a proposal for the compounding of
an offence for a default in compliance with any provision of this
Act which requires a company or its officer to file or register with,
or deliver or send to, the Registrar any return, account or other
document, may, direct, by order, if it or he thinks fit to do so, any
officer or other employee of the company to file or register with,
or on payment of the fee, and the additional fee, required to be paid
under section 611, such return, account or other document within
such time as may be specified in the order.
(6) Any officer or other employee of the Company who fails to
comply with any order made by the Company Law Board or the
Regional Director under sub-section ( 5) shall be punishable with
imprisonment for a term which may extend to six months, or with
file not exceeding fifty thousand rupees, or with both.
(7) Notwithstanding anything contained in the Code of Criminal
Procedure, 1973,—
(a) any offence which is punishable under this Act with
imprisonment or with fine, or with both, shall be compoundable
with the permission of the Court, in accordance with the
procedure laid down in that Act for compounding of offences;
(b) any offence which is punishable under this Act with
imprisonment only or with imprisonment and also with fine shall
not be compoundable.
(8) No offence specified in this section shall be compounded
except under and in accordance with the provisions of this section.

627. (1) If, on an application made to a Judge of a High Court
in chambers by the Public Prosecutor to the State or by the Central
Government, or by a company prosecutor appointed under section
624A. it is shown that there is reasonable cause to believe that any
person has, while he was an officer of a company, committed an
offence in connection with the management of the company’s
affairs, and the that evidence of the commission of the offence is
to be found in any books or papers of or under the control of the
company, an order maybe made—
(i) authorizing any person named therein to inspect the
said books or papers or any of them for the purpose of
investigating and obtaining evidence of the commission of, the
offence; or
(ii) requiring the manager of the company or such other
officer thereof as may be named in the order, to produce the
said books or papers or any of them to a person, and a place
and time, named in the order.
(2) Sub-section ( 1) shall apply also in relation to any books or
papers of a person carrying on the business of banking so far they
relate to the company’s affairs, as it applies to any books or papers
of or under the control of the company, except that no such order
as is referred to in clause ( ii) thereof shall be made by virtue of this
sub-section.
(3) No appeal shall lie from the decision of a Judge of the High
Court under this section.

EGAL P ROCEEDINGS
632. Where a limited company is plaintiff or petitioner in any
suit or other legal proceeding, any Court having jurisdiction in the
matter may, if there is reason to believe that the company will be
unable to pay the costs of defendant if he is successful in his
defence, require sufficient security to be given for those costs, and
may stay all proceedings until the security is given.
*****
634A. Any order made by the Company Law Board maybe
enforced by that Board in the same manner as if it were a decree
made by a Court in a suit pending therein, and it shall be lawful for
that Board to send, in the case of its inability to execute such order,
to the Court within the local limits of whose jurisdiction,—
(a) in the case of an order against a company, the
registered office of the company is situated, or
(b) in the case of an order against any other person, the
person concerned voluntarily resides, or carries on business or
personally works for gain.

635. ( 1) Where any order made by one Court is required to be
enforced by another Court, a certified copy of the order shall be
produced to the proper officer of the Court required to enforce the
order.
(2) The production of such certified copy shall be sufficient
evidence of the order.
(3) Upon the production of such certified copy, the court shall
take the requisite steps for enforcing the order, in the same manner
as if it had been made by itself.
(4) Where any order made by the Company Law Board is
required to be enforced by a Court, a certified copy of the order
shall be produced to the proper officer of the Court required to
enforce the order and the provisions of sub-sections ( 2) and ( 3)
shall, as far as may be, apply to every such order in the same
manner and to the same extent as they apply to an order made by
a Court.

Grant of approval, etc., subject to conditions and levy of fees
on applications
637A. (1) Where the Central Government or Company Law
Board is required or authorized by any provision of this Act,—
(a) to accord approval, sanction, consent, confirmation or
recognition to or in relation to, any matter;
(b) to give any direction in relation to any matter; or
(c) to grant any exemption in relation to any matter,
then, in the absence of anything to the contrary contained in such
or any other provision of this Act, the Central Government or
Company Law Board may accord, give or grant such approval,
sanction, consent, confirmation, recognition, direction or exemption,
subject to such conditions, limitations or restrictions as it may think
fit to impose and may, in the case of contravention of any such
condition, limitation or restriction, rescind or withdraw such
approval, sanction, consent, confirmation, recognition, direction or
exemption.
(2) Save as otherwise expressly provided in this Act, every
application which may be, or it required to be, made to the Central
Government or Company Law Board under any provision of this
Act—
(a) in respect of any approval, sanction, consent,
confirmation or recognition to be accorded by that Government
or board to, or in relation to, any matter; or
(b) in respect of any direction or exemption to be given or
granted by that Government or Board in relation to any matter;

Computation of time for filing orders of Court or the Company Law
Board
640A. Except as expressly provided in this behalf elsewhere in
this Act, where by any provision of this Act, any order of the Court
or the Company Law Board is required to be filed with the
Registrar, or a company or any other person within a period
specified therein, then, in computing that period, the time taken in
drawing up the order and in obtaining a copy thereof shall be
excluded.

as to the reduction of the capital; and

ORM IN WHICH SECTIONS 539 TO 544 OF ACT TO APPLY TO CASES

539. If with intent to defraud or deceive any person, any
officer or member of a company in respect of which an application
has been made under section 397 or 398—
(a) destroys, mutilate alters, falsifies or secrets any books,
papers or securities, or is privy to the destruction, mutilation,
alteration, falsification, or secreting of any books, papers or
securities: or
(b) makes, or is privy to the making of, any false or
fraudulent entry in any register, books of account or document
belonging to the company,
Penalty for
falsification
for books.
65 of 1960.

270
he shall be punishable with imprisonment for a term which may
extend to seven years, and shall also be liable to fine.
540. If any person, being at the time of the commission of the
alleged offence, an officer or a company in repect of which the
Company Law Board subsequently makes an order under section
397 or 398,—
(a) has, by false pretences or by means of any other
fraud, induced any person to give credit to the company;
(b) with intent to defraud creditors of the company, has
made or caused to be made any gift or transfer of or charge
on, or has caused or connived at the levying of any execution
against the property of the company; or
(c) with intent to defraud creditors of the company, has
concealed or removed any part of the property of the company
since the date of any unsatisfied judgment or order for payment
of money obtained against the company, or within two months
before that date,
he shall be punishable with imprisonment for a term which may
extends to two years and shall also be liable to fine.
541. (1) Where an application has been made to the Company
Law Board under section, 397 or 398 in respect of a company, if
it is shown that proper books of account were not kept by the
company throughout the period of two years immediately preceding
the making of the application, or the period between the
incorporation of the company and the making of the application,
which ever is shorter, every officer of the company who is in
default shall, unless he shows that the acted honestly and that in the
circumstances in which the business of the company was carried
on, the default was excusable, be punishable with imprisonment for
a term which may extend to one year.
(2) For the purposes of this section, proper books of account
shall be deemed not to have been kept in the case of any company,
if there have not been kept—
(a) such books of accounts as are necessary to exhibit and
explain the transactions and financial position of the business of
the company, including books containing entries made from day
to day in sufficient detail of all cash received and all cash paid;
and
(b) where the business of the company has involved
dealings in goods, statements of the annual stock takings and
(except in the case of goods sold by way of ordinary retail
trade) of all goods sold and purchased showing the goods and
the buyers and seller thereof in sufficient details to enable those
goods and those buyers and sellers to be identified.

542. (1) If in the course of the proceedings on an application
made to the Company Law Board under section 397 or 398 in
respect of a company, it appears that any business of the company
has been carried on with intent to defraud creditors of the company,
or any other persons, or for any fraudulent purpose, the Company
Law Board may, if it thinks it proper so to do, declare that any
persons who were knowingly parties to the carrying on of the
business in the manner aforesaid shall be personally responsible,
without any limitation of liability, for all or any of the debts or other
liabilities of the company as the Company Law Board may direct.
(2) ( a) Where the Company Law Board makes any such
declaration, it may give such further directions as it thinks proper
for the purpose of giving effect to that declaration.
(b) In particular, the Company Law Board may make
provision for making the liability of any such person under the
declaration a charge on any debt or obligations due from the
company to him, or on any mortgage or charge or any interest
in any mortgage or charge on any assets of the company held
by or vested in him, or any person on his behalf, or any person
claiming as assignee from or through the person liable or any
person acting on his behalf.
(c) the Company Law Board may, from time to time, make
such further orders as may be necessary for the purpose of
enforcing any charge imposed under this sub-section.
(d) For the purpose of this sub-section, the expression
“assignee” includes any person to whom or in whose favour, by
the directions of the person liable, the debt, obligation, mortgage
or charge was created, issued or transferred or the interest was
created, but does not include in an assignee for valuable
consideration (not including consideration by way of marriage)
given in good faith and without notice of any of the matters on
the ground of which the declaration is made.
(3) Where any business of a company is carried on with such
intent or for such purpose as is mentioned in sub-section ( 1), every
person who was knowingly a party to the carrying on of the
business in the manner aforesaid, shall be punishable with
imprisonment for a term which may extend to two years, or with
fine which may extend to five thousand rupees, or with both.
(4) This section shall apply, notwithstanding that the person
concerned may be criminally liable in respect of the matters on the
ground of which the declaration is to be made.
543. ( 1) If, the course of the proceedings on an application
made to the Company Law Board under section 397 or 398, it
appears that any person who has taken part in the promotion or
formation of the company, or any past or present director, managing
agent, secretaries and treasures, manager or officer of the
company—

(a) has misapplied or retained or become liable or
accountable for any money or property of the company; or
(b) has been guilty of any misfeasance or breach of trust
in relation to the company.
The Company Law Board may, on the application of any creditor or
member, examine into the conduct of such person, director,
managing agent, secretaries and treasurers, manager or officer
aforesaid, and compel him to repay or restore the money or
property or any part thereof respectively, with interest at such rate
as the Company Law Board thinks just or to contribute such sum
to the assets of the company by way of compensation in respect of
the misapplication, retainer, misfeasance or breach of trust as the
Company Law Board thinks just.
(2) This section shall apply notwithstanding that the matter is
one for which the person concerned may be criminally liable.
544. Where a declaration under section 542 or an order under
section 543 is or may be made in respect of a firm or body
corporate, the Company Law Board shall also have power to make
a declaration under section 542 or pass an order under section 543,
as the case may be, in respect of any person who is a partner in
that firm or a director of that body corporate.

Sec.2(f)
The term “secretary in whole
time practice” should be
substituted by the term “whole
time secretary or secretary in
whole time practice” because
there may not be many whole
time practicing secretaries.
The definition ‘Industrial
undertaking, needs to be
suitably amended to remain in
line with the existing definition
with exception that ancillary
industrial undertaking is roped
in. The small scale industrial
undertaking continues to
remain out of the definition.
Section may be modified as
follows: “... failed to repay its
debts within any three
consecutive quarters of the
debt falling due or on demand
for its repayment by a creditor
or creditors of such company.”
The amount of debt under
sub-clause (ii) of section
2(46AA) should be specified as
rupees one lakh.
The term ‘demand’ should be
amplified.
This term has been defined in
section 2(45A) of the Companies
Act, 1956. No change is called
for.
Present section 3(f) of the Sick
Industrial Companies (Special
Provisions) Act, 1985 excludes
ancillary industrial undertaking
and small scale units. As such in
the Bill it has been excluded.
The wording of the provision
appears to be sufficient. No
change is desirable.
This provision is meant to provide
protection to small investors/
creditors. Hence specifying any
amount will be detrimental to
them. Suggestion accepted. This
could be covered in rules.

8. Cl. 2 ICAI
Sec.
2(29A)
A sick industrial company is
defined as an industrial
company, which has; (i) the
accumulated losses as equal to
fifty per cent or more of its
average net worth during four
years immediately preceding
such financial years; or (ii)
failed to repay its debts within
any three consecutive quarters
on demand for its repayment
by a creditor or creditors of
such company.
The second criterion does not
bring into focus the debt
default in clear terms. Hence,
sub-section (ii) may be
reframed as follows:
“failed to repay its debt within
two consecutive quarters on
demand for its repayment by a
creditor of such company” .
It is also for consideration
whether consistent incurrence
of business loss by a company
for, say, three years can be
included as one of the criterion
for the sickness.
Definition could incorporate
the wordings “where the
company fails to repay its
debts within three consecutive
quarters from the date on
which it became due and the
amount exceeds Rs. 1 lakh in
the aggregate.
The definition of sickness may
be reworded as “at the end of
any financial year” instead of
“in any financial year”.
The definition of ‘Net Worth’
does not consider practical
situations. There may be cases
where ‘Notes to accounts”
Debt/default includes any default
in payment of dues on the part
of the company on demand by
creditors, depositor etc.
Definition of sickness provided
in the Bill appears to be
sufficient. Reducing the default
period by one quarter of year
will not make any difference.
This has been prescribed on the
basis of the recommendations of
High Level Committee.
Continuous loss for three
consecutive years to a
insignificant level may not erode
the net worth or compel to
default a repayment of debt.
This provision is meant to
provide protection to investors/
creditors as a whole including
small investors and creditors.
No change is called for as the
definition given is sufficiently
worded.
Suggestion agreed to. However, to
deal with the notes to accounts for
non-charging and under charging
of expenses, rules could be made.

285
12 3 4 5
refer to certain non-provisions/
under charging of expenses
etc. Auditors may have also
qualified their reports for such
non-provisions/under charge
expenses. Total effect of the
same on paid up capital and
free reserves be taken into
account for determining the
net worth. The same may
result in eroding fifty-per cent
or entire net worth of the

In the definition of ‘sick

exceeding 3 months.

be in consonance with similar
provision proposed in clause
40(section 434 of the
Company Act, 1956).
As the term ‘demand’ has not
been defined in sub-clause (ii),
it may lead to varied
interpretations such as whether
a normal, collection notice
would be considered as
‘demand’ or a special notice
for demand would be required.
It is, therefore, suggested that
sub-clause (ii) be reworded.
The Bill seeks to provide that
in order to become a sick
industrial company, inter-alia ,
the company’s accumulated
losses should be equal to at
least 50% of its average net
worth. ‘Net Worth’ has been
defined to mean the total of
paid up capital and free
reserves, the question is
whether or not accumulated
losses have to be deducted for
computing the net worth.
The definition of ‘operating
agency’ is extended to include
‘any other person as may be
specified by the Tribunal’
which is very amorphous and
vague. This may be
reconsidered.
In the definition of ‘sick
Industrial Company’, only
erosion of net worth and debt
default are included. Default of
statutory dues relating to the
workers and non-remittance of
deductions made from workers
wages to the LIC, cooperative
society, etc. have not been
included. This also may be
included.
The word Demand used here
denotes demand made orally or
in writing which is accepted as
evidence.
In accordance with the accepted
accounting principles
accumulated losses have to be
deducted for computing net
worth.
This has been done with a view
to provide effective services of
reconstruction can differ in
character from on case to case
basis, and wisdom of Tribunal
can be relied upon.
Non-payment of group insurance
perimium, wages etc. will reflect
in the Balance Sheet of the
company under the head
Outstanding Liability and covered
under the term debt.

2. Cl. 6 ICICI
Sec.
10FQ
(CLAUSE 4)
After the constitution of
Appellate Tribunal, any appeal
against the order of the CLB
should be filed with it and
jurisdiction of High Courts
should be avoided.
The pending cases with CLB
are proposed to be transferred
to the NCLT immediately upon
its constitution. However, the
cases pending before BIFR
would require fresh
presentations to NCLT.
With a view to ensuring
smooth transition from the
BIFR to NCLT, it is suggested
that instead of dismantling
BIFR immediately, it should be
allowed to continue till all the
cases pending with it are
disposed of.
(CLAUSE 6)
Where a Single-Member Bench
has been created, it may be
provided that such single-
member can only be a Judicial
member.
Period of 120 days is
recommended within which
the Appellate Tribunal must
decide on the appeal.
12 3 4 5
Cases pending before the CLB
will be transferred to NCLT
upon its constitution and orders
passed by NCLT will naturally
be appealable to the Appellate
Tribunal. However, orders
already passed by CLB before
constitution of NCLT have to be
appealed in the High Court.
The very purpose of repealing
SICA is to bring cases of sick
companies before the speedier
processes prescribed by NCLT.
Continuation of BIFR will defeat
this purpose.
Tribunal will authorise its
Members in its behalf to
function as single member
bench. Therefore, the member
may be Judicial or Technical
depending upon the issue to be
decided by the Bench at the
relevant point of time.
Section 10FQ(6) provides six
months for disposal of the
appeal keeping in mind the
practical difficulties. Too short a
period may be ambitious, and not
workable, providing six months
time is justified. No further
reduction of time is desirable.

The President and any other
member of the Tribunal should
be eligible for reappointment
for another term of three
years.
A provision should be made to
the effect that no injunctions
are passed by any Court
against sale of assets.
The Central Government
should be empowered to
appoint as many members as
may be considered necessary.
Section 10FE does not
specifically provide for
reappointment of the President
and members if the term
comes to an end and they had
not reached the specified
upper age limit. On the other
hand Section 10FT specifically
states that the Chairperson and
members of the Appellate
Tribunal shall be eligible for
reappointment for another term
of three years. In order to
encourage qualified and
experienced persons even at
young age to become the
President and members of the
Tribunal, it is necessary that
there should be an express
provision for reappointment on
the expiry of the term of three
years if they have not crossed
the upper age limit for holding
the office. Section 10FE may
be suitably amended to give
effect to the above.
Re-appointment of President and
other Members have not been
prohibited in the Act.
Section 10GB provides that no
civil court shall have jurisdiction
to entertain any suit or
proceeding in respect of any
matter which the Tribunal or the
Appellate Tribunal is empowered
with. This includes sale of assets
also.
It is a legislative requirement to
fix an upper limit of members.
Blanket power to appoint any
number of Members any not be
desirable.
Re-appointment of President or
other Members have not been
barred in the Act. They can be
re-appointed if they are selected
by the Selection Committee.

bankruptcy – sell assets first
as quickly as possible and

the companies.

secured debt. The level fixed
under the Act could be

audited financial statement.

be 5 per cent of the secured
and unsecured debts taken
together from the latest audited
financial statement.

not arise.

secured and unsecured creditors.

securities of the company,

interest are not protected in the
same way. This provision is
made to take care the interest of
the unsecured and small
depositors/creditors also.
In view of above no amount has
been prescribed for repayment
of debts.

work to complete their task.

as a last resort. Therefore,
creation of two separate bodies

not necessary.

there is extensive delay in
concluding winding up
proceedings. Actually, a delay
occures only after the winding
up order is posted due to

All the cases pending with BIFR
are to be abated.
Some SICA provisions are open
ended without linking by time-
frame. The new Bill envisages a
time-frame of 240 to 390 days
for revival/rehabilitation. A
detailed note on this aspect has
been submitted to the Committee
separately.
The intention of the Bill is to
bring the dispute resolutions,
Corporate restructuring and
winding up under one roof.
Excessive pendency before CLB
is not the only reason for the
new Bill.
Experiences have shown
obtaining an order from Courts
takes its own time. The
proposed Bill provides for 62
members, 10 special benches
double and single member

of a statutory fixed commission
not exceeding 5%. This
amendment will be sufficient to
expedite the recovery.

Tribunal:
The existing tribunals are all
concerned with cases wherein
State or Central Government is
one of the adversarial party.
Therefore, ITAT, CEGAT, CAT,
SAT, etc., are all examples
where the Central or State
Government on one side and
the assesses or the individual
persons on the other.
There is no necessity of
appoinment of Technical
Member in the Tribunal as there
is no specialised technical
knowledge which is required for
a technical member.
Objects regarding National
Company Law Tribunal:
It is virtually impossible to
recruit 62 members unless
most of them are retired
Government servants.
Definition and qualification of
judicial and technical members
includes Central Government
employees in the Indian
Company Law Service and
Indian Legal Service, bank
employees etc. only 15 years
service is required, presiding
officer of a Labour Court
qualified under the category of
Technology Member rather
than Judicial member is not
understandable.
Tribunals given here are diectly
related to Central/State
Government. Therefore, in
obvious manner Central/State
Government is one of the party
in cases filed in these Tribunals.
In the National Company Law
Tribunal Government is not a
party for any obvious reasons.
Therefore, it ought to be non-
Government parties either way.
The Tribunal is proposed to be
constituted with judicial and
technical members to deal with
cases where the expertise of
judicial and technical field are
required.
Government w ill endeavour to
appoint all the members.
In the Indian Legal Service officer
at the level of Joint Secretary or
in the Indian Company Law
Service of similar rank have been
included for their experience in
this specialized field. Presently
Regional Director in Indian
Company Law Service are vested
with power of compounding of
fines under section 621 of the
Companies Act. This is a similar
power which the CLB is entrusted
with. The Presiding Officer in
Labour Court is qualified to be
appointed as technical member to
deal with the cases where labour
interest is involved.
